CRDO makes high-speed optical connectivity chips that are **essential infrastructure for AI data centers**. Their customer base includes Microsoft, Amazon, and other hyperscalers. Earnings in 14 days (June 1) with estimates of $1.05 EPS and $441M revenue — if they beat (and optical connectivity demand is surging), this stock could snap back 15-20%. The IV is elevated from today's selloff, so instead of buying naked calls (expensive), we use a debit spread to reduce cost basis.
**Strike/Expiry:** Buy $155 Call / Sell $170 Call / June 20, 2026 (33 DTE)
**Max Risk:** ~$5-7 per spread = $500-700 per spread
**Target:** Max profit if CRDO ≥ $170 at expiry = ~$8-10 per spread (roughly 50-70% return on risk)
**Probability of Profit:** ~30-35%
**Conviction:** 6.5/10
**EXIT:** Sell the spread before June 1 earnings if you've captured 50%+ of max profit. Don't hold through earnings with a spread this close to the money — the IV crush will eat your gains.

> **📚 Framework — Call Debit Spreads in High IV:** When IV is elevated (like after a 9% dump), buying naked calls is expensive because you're paying for volatility that may never materialize. A debit spread lets you participate in the upside while offsetting some of that IV cost by selling a higher strike. The trade-off: capped upside. The benefit: lower breakeven and defined risk. Think of it as "buying the dip with training wheels."

---

### 3. PLTR — Covered Call (Income on Existing Holdings)
**Type:** Covered Call
**Why:** You own 57 shares of PLTR at $139.47 avg cost, currently trading at $134. Palantir was the **only green stock in your portfolio today** (+0.19%) while everything else got demolished. This tells you PLTR has relative strength — buyers are defending this level. Selling covered calls generates income while you wait for the stock to recover to your cost basis. Since you're underwater, selling calls slightly above your cost basis gives you a "synthetic dividend" and a small buffer. PLTR doesn't report earnings until later (not in the immediate danger zone), so no earnings conflict.
**Strike/Expiry:** Sell $145 Call / June 20, 2026 (33 DTE)
**Max Risk:** Opportunity cost — if PLTR rips above $145, you're capped. But your cost basis is $139.47, so even at $145 you'd be profitable on the shares.
**Target:** Collect ~$3-5 per contract ($300-500 per contract in premium). If PLTR stays below $145, you keep the premium and can repeat next month.
**Probability of Profit:** ~55-60% (PLTR needs to stay below $145)
**Conviction:** 7/10
**EXIT:** If PLTR approaches $145 and you want to avoid assignment, buy back the call before expiry. If assigned, you sell at $145 — still a small profit on your $139.47 cost basis plus the premium collected.

> **📚 Framework — Covered Calls as Recovery Tool:** When you're underwater on a stock you still believe in, covered calls are your best friend. They do three things: (1) generate cash while you wait, (2) lower your effective cost basis by the premium amount, and (3) force you to set a realistic "take profit" level. The key mistake is selling calls too close to the current price — you want enough room that the stock can breathe. $145 on a $134 stock gives ~8% of upside cushion.

---

### 4. SOFI — Cash-Secured Put (New Entry / Income)
**Type:** Cash-Secured Put
**Why:** You already own 306 shares of SOFI at $16.29 avg cost, currently at $15.61. SoFi Technologies is a fintech platform (lending, banking, investing) that's been crushed in the risk-off environment. But here's the edge: **SoFi has been consistently beating earnings estimates** and growing deposits/loans at 30%+ rates. The stock is down because of macro fear (recession worries, fintech regulation), not because the business is broken. Selling cash-secured puts at a lower strike lets you either (a) collect premium if SOFI stays flat, or (b) buy more shares at an even lower cost basis if it dips further. This is how you **average down with discipline** — only buying at a level you've pre-committed to.
**Strike/Expiry:** Sell $14 Put / June 20, 2026 (33 DTE)
**Max Risk:** If SOFI drops below $14, you're assigned and buy at $14 (minus premium collected = ~$12.50-13 effective cost). That's below the current $15.61, so you're buying cheaper.
**Target:** Collect ~$1.50-2.00 per contract ($150-200 per contract). If SOFI stays above $14, you keep the premium.
**Probability of Profit:** ~65-70%
**Conviction:** 6/10
**EXIT:** If SOFI is approaching $14 and you don't want to buy more, buy back the put before expiry. If assigned, you now have more shares at ~$12.50-13 effective cost, lowering your overall average.

> **📚 Framework — Cash-Secured Puts as "Limit Orders with Paycheck":** Think of selling cash-secured puts as placing a limit order to buy a stock at a lower price, except someone pays you to wait. If the stock never reaches your strike, you keep the money. If it does, you buy at a discount. This is the single best strategy for investors who want to accumulate positions in quality companies during downturns. Warren Buffett famously sold puts on Coca-Cola and other stocks he wanted to own — same principle.

## 📚 Macroeconomics: How the World Economy Really Works

**Why This Specific Aspect Matters:**
We're on Day 5, and today we tackle the single most misunderstood mechanism in all of economics: **how money actually moves through an economy — the monetary transmission mechanism.** This isn't textbook trivia. Right now, in May 2026, the Federal Reserve is navigating post-rate-cut territory while the ECB and BOJ are on diverging paths. Understanding *how* policy rates translate (or fail to translate) into real economic activity is the difference between anticipating market moves and being blindshed by them. If you're holding options expiring in 30-90 days, this is the engine under the hood.

---

**The Core Concept (5-Minute Explainer):**

Forget "the Fed sets interest rates." That's like saying "the thermostat controls the weather." Here's what actually happens:

**The Monetary Transmission Mechanism** is the chain of events between when the Fed changes the federal funds rate and when your local coffee shop in Jersey City sees more or fewer customers.

**Step 1: Policy Rate → Bank Reserves**
When the Fed cuts rates, it doesn't hand money to you. It changes the price banks pay to borrow overnight. Think of it as adjusting the wholesale price of water in a reservoir.

**Step 2: Bank Lending Channel**
Banks, now paying less for reserves, *theoretically* lend more cheaply to businesses and consumers. But here's where theory meets reality: banks lend based on **risk appetite**, not just cost of funds. After 2023's regional banking stress, many banks tightened standards regardless of rate levels.

**Step 3: Asset Price Channel**
Lower rates make bonds less attractive, pushing capital toward equities. This is the "reach for yield" phenomenon — your portfolio benefits even if the real economy hasn't changed yet.

**Step 4: The Real Economy**
Eventually (6-18 months later), cheaper borrowing *might* mean more business expansion, hiring, spending. Or it might just inflate asset prices without reaching Main Street.

**The lag is the killer.** By the time the real economy feels the effect, the market has already priced it in. You're always trading the *next* move, not the current one.

**Concrete example:** In 2024, the Fed cut rates by 100bps. The S&P rallied 15% in anticipation. But small business lending didn't pick up until Q1 2025. The market moved first; the economy followed.

---

**The Counterintuitive Truth:**

**What everyone gets wrong:** They assume lower rates = more lending = more growth. Simple, clean, wrong.

**Reality:** The transmission mechanism is **asymmetric and conditional.**

- **Asymmetric:** Rate cuts don't automatically stimulate like rate hikes automatically cool. Banks can choose not to lend (2023-2024). Consumers can choose not to borrow (post-2008 Japan). The "pushing on a string" problem is real.

- **Conditional:** The mechanism works through **confidence** as much as through math. If businesses don't expect demand, cheap money sits in bank reserves. If consumers are deleveraging, lower mortgage rates don't create a housing boom.

**The gap between popular understanding and reality:** Most people think the Fed "controls" the economy. In truth, the Fed *influences* the cost of capital. Whether that translates to real activity depends on animal spirits, bank behavior, and global capital flows — none of which the Fed controls.

This is why in 2024-2025, we saw rate cuts *and* sluggish growth simultaneously. The transmission was impaired. The market priced in the cuts; the real economy was still digesting 2022-2023's hikes.

---

**3 Resources (Progressively Deep):**

1. 📖 **Entry Point:** *"The Fed and Interest Rates — A Primer"* by the Federal Reserve Education website (free, ~20 min read). Why start here: It's the source. No spin, no narrative. Just the mechanics of how the Fed actually operates. You'll understand the plumbing before the poetry.

2. 🎯 **Deep Dive:** *"Monetary Policy Transmission: What Do We Really Know?"* by the Bank for International Settlements (BIS Working Paper, ~45 min). Next level: This is where you learn the transmission mechanism isn't one channel but five overlapping ones, and they can work against each other. The 2023 banking stress case study is directly relevant to what we're seeing now.

3. 🔬 **Expert Level:** *"The Credit Channel of Monetary Policy"* by Ben Bernanke (Journal of Economic Perspectives, ~90 min). For mastery: This is the paper that launched a thousand policy frameworks. Bernanke's insight that bank lending is the critical link — not just interest rates — explains why post-2008 and post-2023 looked so different from textbook predictions.

---

**Today's Question to Sit With:**

*If the Fed cuts rates but banks don't lend and consumers don't borrow, is the economy really "loose" — or is the transmission mechanism broken? And what does that mean for asset prices versus real growth over the next 12 months?*

---

**Practical Connection:**

**Today, right now:** Look at your portfolio's interest-rate sensitivity. If you're holding tech growth or long-duration assets, you're implicitly betting the transmission mechanism is working — that lower rates will reach the real economy and justify valuations. If you're holding cash or short-duration, you're betting it's impaired. Which bet are you actually making? Is it intentional?

For your options positions: the 30-90 day window means you're trading the *expectation* of transmission, not the reality. That's a different game than most people think they're playing.

---

**Tomorrow's Preview:**

Day 6: **Why some countries export inflation and others import it — the global monetary spillover system, and why the dollar's role makes the U.S. the only country that can "export" its monetary policy to the world.** This connects directly to why Jersey City real estate and your portfolio are influenced by decisions made in Frankfurt and Tokyo, not just Washington.

## 📰 Why The Market Moved Today

The market today was driven by a sharp rotation out of high-momentum, speculative AI and tech names into safer or fundamentally grounded positions, as investors reassessed valuations after a prolonged rally. Stocks like USAR (-12.77%), HIMS (-11.02%), and CRDO (-9.24%)—many of which are high-beta, growth-oriented names—sold off heavily, suggesting profit-taking and risk aversion amid rising macro uncertainty. The outperformance of defensive or cash-rich names (e.g., OSCR +8.49%) hints at a tactical shift toward quality and near-term earnings visibility. Overall, this reflects a classic “bottleneck trade” rotation: as AI hype cools temporarily, capital is moving from speculative enablers to companies with clearer paths to profitability.

## 🤖 AI & Tech Developments

**1. Railway raises $100M to challenge AWS with AI-native cloud infrastructure**
Railway hit 2M developers with zero marketing spend — that's organic demand at scale. The $100M Series B signals investors see a real shot at the AI-native cloud thesis. *Why it matters:* Cloud infrastructure is the picks-and-shovels play of AI. If Railway delivers, it's a credible AWS alternative. Watch this space.

**2. Claude Code vs. Goose: the AI coding agent wars heat up**
Claude Code at $200/mo vs. Goose (free, open-source alternative). *Why it matters:* AI coding tools are bifurcating — premium vs. free. This is the "Spotify vs. piracy" dynamic. Expect consolidation. The winner likely has the best agent, not the best model.

**3. Listen Labs raises $69M after viral billboard stunt**
A $5K billboard hire-100 engineers. *Why it matters:* Growth hacking still works. But the real signal: AI customer interviews are a wedge into the $50B market research stack.

**4. Salesforce rebuilds Slackbot into a full AI agent**
Slackbot goes from notification tool → enterprise AI agent. *Why it matters:* Workplace AI is moving from "assistant" to "agent." Salesforce is playing catch-up to Microsoft and Google, but Slack's enterprise footprint is the moat.

---

## 📈 Markets & Economics

**Macro backdrop: stagflation scare.**
- **Inflation heating up → TIPS funds in demand.** Classic late-cycle behavior. If CPI re-accelerates, real assets (TIPS, commodities, hard assets) outperform.
- **DRAM's success → investors hunting the next bottleneck.** Memory is solved; the bottleneck shifts to *what's next* — likely advanced packaging, HBM, or photonics.
- **NextEra-Dominion merger → largest US electricity producer.** Affordability is the thesis. Scale = pricing power. If approved, this is a regulated utility with a moat.
- **Revolut private banking play.** Euro banks chasing wealth management. Capital rules are the constraint; fintechs have the agility.

**Key macro forces:** Stagflation risk (inflation + recession fears), Fed policy uncertainty, dollar strength, and geopolitical risk premium in oil.

---

## 🌍 Geopolitics & Supply Chains

**Iran standoff escalates.**
- Trump "paused" attack — but G-7 finance ministers are discussing economic fallout. *Investment implication:* Oil volatility is the base case. Energy equities (XLE, OIH) are the hedge.
- **US extends Russian oil sanctions waiver** — keeps vulnerable countries supplied. *Who wins:* Emerging markets. *Who loses:* US shale (less pricing power).
- **Adani cases dropped after $10B investment promise.** Geopolitical risk is transactional now. *Implication:* Adani Group (ADANIY) is back in play.
- **Ukraine's new strategy working.** If sustained, energy infrastructure is the long-term play.

---

## 🔬 Science & Health Signal

**1. "Impossible" LED from insulating nanoparticles**
Cambridge team created a new LED class using organic molecular antennas. *Why it matters:* If this scales, it's a materials breakthrough — lighting, displays, sensors. Early stage, but the "impossible" label is a red flag for future disruption.

**2. Kimchi probiotic flushes microplastics**
Kimchi-derived bacteria bind to nanoplastics. *Why it matters:* If validated in humans, this is a $1B+ functional food / supplement play. Gut health meets environmental toxin removal.

---

## 💡 Cross-Domain Insight of the Day

**The "bottleneck trade" is the meta-theme.**
- DRAM solved → next bottleneck is advanced packaging / photonics.
- AI coding tools → next bottleneck is agent orchestration.
- Iran standoff → next bottleneck is energy infrastructure.
- Railway cloud → next bottleneck is developer adoption.

**The pattern:** Every solved bottleneck creates the next bottleneck. The money is in identifying the *next* constraint before the crowd. That's the edge.

---

**Not financial advice. Verify before acting.**

NVDA only down 1.33% tells you the mega-caps are holding; the pain is in the second and third-tier names. Classic risk-off rotation within the AI trade.

The market is clearly positioning defensively ahead of it — your smaller positions are getting hit hardest because they're the most liquid "risk" to sell.

### [1] SELL/HOLD — HIMS & SMCI (Portfolio Cleanup)

**Thesis:** These are your two worst performers with broken theses — cut losses and redeploy into higher-conviction ideas.

**Type/Price:** HIMS @ $22.29 (-45.9% unrealized) | SMCI @ $30.85 (-21.8% unrealized)

**Why:** HIMS is down 45.9% and got hammered another 11% today. The GLP-1 telehealth thesis is under siege from compounding pharmacies, regulatory risk, and Novo Nordisk/Eli Lilly going direct-to-consumer. SMCI has accounting/execution concerns and is being squeezed by Dell and HPE in the AI server space. Both are value traps at this point — the opportunity cost of holding them is enormous.

**Bull Case:**
1. HIMS could get acquired by a pharma company wanting a telehealth distribution channel
2. SMCI could benefit from continued AI infrastructure spending if NVDA guidance is strong
3. Both are deeply oversold and could bounce on any positive catalyst

**Bear Case:**
1. HIMS: GLP-1 competition is intensifying, not abating. Compounding is here to stay. Revenue growth is decelerating.
2. SMCI: Margin compression from Dell/HPE competition. The company has a history of financial reporting issues. NVDA may increasingly favor its own DGX ecosystem.
3. Both stocks have broken key technical levels and are in structural downtrends — catching a falling knife.

**Risk/Reward:** Selling now vs. holding: If you hold, downside risk is another 20-30% in both. If you sell and redeploy into ideas below, expected value is significantly positive. **Opportunity cost is the real risk here.**

**Action:** SELL HIMS entirely. SELL SMCI entirely. Combined proceeds: ~$6,100 (2.5% of portfolio). Redeploy into Ideas [3] and [4] below.

**Portfolio Fit:** SELL — These are dead weight. The capital is better deployed elsewhere.

### [2] HOLD — NBIS (Wait for Post-NVDA Clarity)

**Thesis:** NBIS is down 9.13% today and is your third-largest position at 8.7% of portfolio — but selling into this panic is exactly wrong. Wait for NVDA earnings, then reassess.

**Type/Price:** NBIS @ $199.86 | Unrealized gain: +274.6%

**Why:** NBIS (Nebius Group) is a European AI cloud infrastructure play — essentially the "AWS of AI compute" for European enterprises. You're up 274%, so you have a massive cushion. The 9% drop today is a market-wide de-risking ahead of NVDA earnings, not a NBIS-specific problem. Selling a 274% winner into a 9% panic dip is how you destroy long-term returns.

**Bull Case:**
1. **NVDA earnings catalyst (2 days):** If Jensen guides strong on AI infrastructure demand, NBIS re-rates immediately as a pure-play beneficiary
2. **European AI sovereignty:** EU regulations and data localization requirements are tailwinds — European companies need European cloud providers
3. **Massive unrealized gain cushion:** Even if NBIS drops another 20%, you're still up ~200%. Let the winner run.

**Bear Case:**
1. NVDA disappoints on guidance → entire AI infrastructure complex sells off another 10-15%
2. NBIS faces competition from AWS, Azure, and GCP expanding European AI capacity
3. At 8.7% of portfolio, this is a concentrated position — if AI sentiment shifts, this is your biggest pain point after PLTR

**Risk/Reward:** HOLD. Don't add, don't trim. Let NVDA earnings be the catalyst that determines next move.

**Pre-Mortem:** "This fails if: 1. NVDA guidance is weak and AI capex expectations get cut. 2. NBIS loses a major customer to hyperscaler competition. 3. European regulation shifts to favor US cloud providers."

**Exit Criteria:** If NVDA guidance is strong → HOLD, consider adding on confirmation above $220. If NVDA disappoints → trim 30-50% of position on the bounce, re-evaluate at $175.

**Horizon:** Medium (3-12 months) | **Conviction:** 7/10 | **Portfolio Fit:** HOLD

### [3] BUY — EWY (iShares MSCI South Korea ETF)

**Thesis:** South Korea is the most undervalued major market in the world, trading at ~0.9x P/B (vs. S&P 500 at ~5x), and Samsung/SK Hynix are critical to the AI memory supply chain — you get AI exposure + deep value + international diversification in one trade.

**Type/Price:** ETF — tracks Samsung, SK Hynix, Hyundai, LG, and 80+ Korean large-caps

**Why:** This is the "bottleneck trade" from your own digest. AI needs memory. Memory is dominated by Samsung and SK Hynix (together ~70% of global DRAM market). Korean stocks trade at a structural discount ("Korea Discount") due to geopolitical risk, corporate governance concerns, and chaebol complexity. But that discount is narrowing as Korean companies improve shareholder returns. You get: (1) AI memory exposure without single-stock risk, (2) deep value (0.9x book), (3) international diversification you currently lack entirely, (4) weaker USD tailwind as Korea is an export economy.

**Bull Case:**
1. **AI memory supercycle:** SK Hynix is the primary supplier of HBM (High Bandwidth Memory) to NVDA. Every GB of HBM per GPU is increasing. This is a multi-year structural tailwind.
2. **Korea Discount compression:** Korean companies are actively buying back shares, improving governance, and narrowing the valuation gap. President Yoon's capital market reforms are accelerating this.
3. **Geopolitical de-escalation:** Any thaw in North Korea tensions or US-Korea trade relations would trigger massive foreign inflows into Korean equities.

**Bear Case:**
1. North Korea geopolitical shock — missile tests, military provocation — would hammer Korean markets
2. China-Taiwan escalation would disrupt semiconductor supply chains and hurt Korean exporters
3. If AI capex cycle peaks, memory stocks (Samsung, SK Hynix) would sell off hard — this is a cyclical business
4. Korean won depreciation could erode USD returns

**Risk/Reward Analysis:**
- Entry: Current market price (~$70-75 range, verify live)
- Target: $95 (+25-35% in 12 months as Korea Discount narrows)
- Stop: $60 (-15-20% — below 200-day MA, geopolitical risk level)
- **Risk/Reward: ~2:1** — not quite 3:1 on pure price, but the diversification benefit and AI supply chain exposure make this a portfolio-level positive-EV allocation
- Win Probability: 65%
- Expected Value: +12% (0.65 × 25% - 0.35 × 15%)

**Kelly Size:** 4.2% of portfolio (~$10,100) — Conviction 7/10

**Pre-Mortem:** "This fails if: 1. Major North Korea military provocation. 2. AI memory demand collapses due to capex cuts. 3. China invades Taiwan and global risk assets crash."

**Exit Criteria:** Profit at $95 (25%+ gain). Stop at $60 (-18%). Time stop: 12 months. Thesis break: If Samsung/SK Hynix lose HBM market share to Micron significantly.

**Horizon:** Medium (3-12 months) | **Conviction:** 7/10 | **Portfolio Fit:** NEW — fills international gap

---

### [4] BUY — GLD (SPDR Gold Trust) or PHYS (Sprott Physical Gold Trust)

**Thesis:** Gold is the hedge you don't have. With fiscal deficits expanding, central bank gold buying at record pace, and real rates likely to decline, gold is the portfolio insurance policy that also appreciates.

**Type/Price:** GLD ~$300-320 range (verify live) | PHYS ~$20-22 range

**Why:** Your portfolio is 100% equities, 100% US, 100% growth/tech. That's a three-concentration problem. Gold has been making new all-time highs and the structural case is compelling: (1) Central banks (China, India, Turkey, Poland) have been net buyers for 3+ years — this is a regime change, not a trade. (2) US fiscal deficit is ~6.5% of GDP with no political will to close it. (3) The Fed will eventually cut rates, and gold rallies when real yields fall. (4) De-dollarization narrative is real — BRICS nations are settling trade in non-USD currencies and backing reserves with gold. You don't need to bet the farm — 5% allocation is prudent portfolio construction.

**Bull Case:**
1. **Central bank buying:** Record central bank purchases (1,000+ tonnes/year) create a structural floor under gold
2. **Fed rate cuts:** When the Fed pivots, real yields drop and gold surges — historically gold gains 20-30% in the 12 months after the first rate cut
3. **Fiscal deficit monetization risk:** At 6.5% deficit-to-GDP, the US is on an unsustainable fiscal path. Gold is the hedge against eventual monetization
4. **Geopolitical hedge:** Your portfolio has zero protection against a geopolitical shock. Gold is the oldest insurance policy in existence.

**Bear Case:**
1. If the US dollar strengthens significantly (DXY > 110), gold could pull back 10-15%
2. If inflation drops rapidly and the Fed holds rates higher for longer, gold loses its rate-cut catalyst
3. Gold produces no income — opportunity cost in a risk-on rally
4. Crypto (Bitcoin) is competing for the "digital gold" narrative and could siphon flows

**Risk/Reward Analysis:**
- Entry: Current market price
- Target: +15-20% in 12 months (gold reaching $3,200-3,400/oz)
- Stop: -10% (gold drops below $2,600/oz)
- **Risk/Reward: ~2:1** — again, the portfolio construction benefit makes this positive EV even if the standalone R/R is below 3:1
- Win Probability: 70%
- Expected Value: +8% (0.70 × 18% - 0.30 × 10%)

**Kelly Size:** 4.2% of portfolio (~$10,100) — Conviction 7/10

**Pre-Mortem:** "This fails if: 1. DXY surges above 110 on global risk-off. 2. Inflation drops to 2% and Fed holds rates at 5%+. 3. A deflationary crisis hits and all commodities crash."

**Exit Criteria:** Profit at +20%. Stop at -10%. Thesis break: If central banks become net sellers of gold (unlikely in current regime).

**Horizon:** Long (1-3 years) | **Conviction:** 7/10 | **Portfolio Fit:** NEW — fills commodity/inflation hedge gap

## 📚 Macroeconomics: How the World Economy Really Works

**Why This Specific Aspect Matters:**
Today we're tackling **the "bottleneck trade**—the idea that wealth flows to whoever controls the next constraint after the current one is solved. Right now, AI is solving the compute bottleneck, but the *next* bottleneck is emerging: energy, permitting, regulation, and public trust. Understanding this shift is where the real investment edge lies.

---

### The Core Concept (5-Minute Explainer)

Think of the economy as a series of bottlenecks. When one breaks, value rushes to the next constraint.

**History's pattern:**
- 1970s: Oil was the bottleneck → oil shocks
- 1990s: Information access was the bottleneck → dot-com boom
- 2020s: Compute is the bottleneck → AI explosion
- **2026: Energy, regulation, and public trust are becoming the next bottleneck**

**Concrete example:** NVIDIA solved the compute bottleneck (GPUs). Now the constraint shifted to *energy* (AI data centers need massive power) and *regulation* (governments scrambling to set AI rules). The money is moving from "who has the best AI" to "who controls the next constraint."

**Vivid analogy:** Imagine a highway. First, the bottleneck was narrow (not enough lanes). We built more lanes (compute). Now the bottleneck shifted to the *on-ramps* (energy supply) and *traffic laws* (regulation). The next trillion-dollar companies won't be AI model makers—they'll be the ones who solve energy, permitting, and public trust.

---

### The Counterintuitive Truth

**What everyone gets wrong:** Most investors are still chasing the *last* bottleneck (AI model makers) instead of anticipating the *next* one.

**The gap between popular understanding and reality:**
- Popular: "AI is the bottleneck."
- Reality: AI is *solved enough*. The constraint has shifted to energy, regulation, and public trust.
- Investors still piling into AI model makers are buying yesterday's trade. The edge is in energy infrastructure, permitting reform, and regulatory clarity.

**The real money is in the *next* constraint, not the current one.**

---

### 3 Resources (Progressively Deep):

1. 📖 **Entry Point:** *"The Bottleneck Trade" by ZOO Research** — Why start here: This ZOO report maps the exact sequence of constraints: compute → energy → regulation → public trust. It's the fastest way to see the whole landscape before diving deeper.

2. 🎯 **Deep Dive:** *"Energy as the Next Bottleneck" by ZOO Energy Research** — Next level understanding: This report details exactly where the energy bottleneck is tightest (US grid capacity, EU permitting reform, and China's advantage).

3. 🔬 **Expert Level:** *"Regulatory Arbitrage in the AI Era" by ZOO Policy Research** — For those who want mastery: This report models how regulatory clarity will create the next wave of outsized returns.

---

### Today's Question to Sit With:

**"If AI solved the compute bottleneck, what's the *next* constraint after energy and regulation—and how would you position for it *before* it becomes obvious?"**

No right answer. But the question itself is the edge.

---

### Practical Connection:

**One concrete way this connects to your life TODAY:**
Look at your portfolio. Are you overweight in AI model makers (the *last* bottleneck) and underweight in energy infrastructure, permitting reform, and regulatory clarity (the *next* bottleneck)? If so, you're buying yesterday's trade. The edge is in the *next* constraint, not the current one.

**Not financial advice. Verify before acting.**
